# Assumption register

Every value in the port that is not yet taken from the original game, with the
lead for replacing it. Engine addresses are in the 3dfx edition (MW2.DLL); the
DOS MW2.EXE has the same simulation. Status: **assumed** (my number),
**inferred** (fits the data, mechanism not traced), **engine** (taken from code).

## 1. Movement and controls (tools/glview.c, src/mech3d.c, src/msim.c)

### Engine findings so far (3dfx MW2.DLL)
- Object classes: table at `0x1024c330`, 32-byte entries. Mech-like classes (1, 2, 4, 5, 6, 8)
  share: init `0x1001b120` (not decompiled; calls `0x1001b200`), reset `0x100190d0`,
  `0x10019310` (26 KB, likely physics), controller update `0x1001a180`, `0x1001adf0`.
- Object: input block at +0x4c (player: `0x10159e50`; AI: object+0x1aa), controller at +0x20.
- Input (done through 2026-10-19 incl. "Player torso servo" / Keypad 5).
- Throttle presets: 10 steps (STOP, 2..9, FULL); input +8 = step / 576 (FULL = 1/64).
- Controller (c[n] = 32-bit field n):
  - c[0x12] throttle target = 1/64 + input+8 x c[0x2c]; c[0x2c] = 1.0 for every mech (reset).
  - c[0x13] follows c[0x12]; speed c[10] = (c[0x13] - 1/64) x c[0x22]; reverse x -0.5.
  - c[0x22] per-mech speed factor = MEK walk MP x 105.4945 (loadout applier `0x10041410`,
    store at `0x10041ac5`), then divided at reset by `DAT_1024a98c` = `DAT_1024a988` x 33.7838,
    which is 1.0 at the default 0.0296 s tick (~33.8 Hz): a frame-time normaliser.
    Full throttle: c[10] = walk MP x 105.49 / 64 = walk MP x 1.648 per base tick. How c[10]
    becomes ground motion is NOT YET TRACED (taken as units/tick it is only ~10 km/h for a
    Timber Wolf, so it is probably not a plain velocity; it may drive the walk animation). (superseded: see "Speed per walking MP" row below: 182 Hz ticks, walk MP x 300 cm/s)
  - twist c[2] = input+4, clamped to +-c[0x38] (per-mech limit). SOURCE NOT YET FOUND. (superseded: see "Torso twist limit / rate" row below (MGEO angle -> c[0x38]))
  - leg turn rate c[0xe] = (input+0xc / 1024 x 90) x cos(min(80, c[0xb] x 6.516) deg).
  - FEET_TO_TORSO (case 0x11): legs turn at a fixed +-55 to meet the torso.
- Loadout applier `0x10041410` also sets: c[0x39] (+0xe4) = tonnage (MEK +0); c[0x2a] (+0xa8)
  = MEK +0x10; c[0x32] (+0xc8) = MEK +0x14; c[0x31] (+0xc4) = jump MP (MEK +8);
  c[0x30] (+0xc0) = 0x712 if jump jets fitted (-2 / -1 otherwise); c[0x3b] (+0xec) =
  "jet ddy" = jump MP / (stack value, probably tonnage) x 0.1184 (debug print label).
- Heat sinking (engine): c[0x27] (+0x9c) = MEK heat_sinking (+0xc) x factor:
  AI mechs 0.000763 (mission temperature < -30: 0.00153, > 50: 0.000687);
  player by difficulty (`DAT_1024ac6c`+5): 0 -> 0.00107 (cold 0.00214); 1 -> 0.000763
  (cold 0.00153, hot 0.000687); 2 -> 0.000687 (cold 0.00103, hot 0.000549).
  Temperature `DAT_1024a9a8` comes from the mission. How c[0x27] is applied per tick (it
  also appears in c[0x25] += (c[0x13] - 1/64) x c[0x27] x 6.4) is NOT YET TRACED. (superseded: see §2 heat model and §20 "Walking heat" (once per frame))
- Speed readout (`0x10018470`, "%d kph"): prints |c[0x3d..0x3f]| (controller +0xf4/f8/fc),
  negated when c[0xb] (+0x2c) < 0. That vector is the velocity, IN KM/H.
- Physics `0x10019310`: velocity integrates an acceleration, v += a x dt, and the move is
  (v + a x dt / 2) x dt handed to the move/collide routine `0x1000b5e0`; dt is an integer
  elapsed time. Branch read so far is the airborne/jump-jet one (uses "jet ddy" c[0x3b]
  and the strafe/flags at input +0x1e..+0x21). The grounded branch that turns throttle
  speed c[10] into acceleration is lines ~140-296 of the decompile, NOT YET READ. (superseded: see §18 "Top speed vs gravity", "Contact radius / climb", "Directional jet drag" (0x10019310 read); the speed response itself is still open, see the "Throttle model" row)
- Elapsed time `DAT_1024ab4c` = game clock `DAT_1024ab44` delta per frame (`0x10008103`),
  x8 or /4 in debug time-scale modes; clock fed from timeGetTime / QueryPerformanceCounter.
  Its unit (ms or a game tick) is NOT YET PINNED - it decides km/h <-> world units. (superseded: see "Speed per walking MP" row (Miles timer 182 Hz))
- Alternative if static tracing stalls: run the DOS game in DOSBox with its debugger and
  read the controller fields live (top speed per mech, twist limit, heat).
- Next: c[0x38] twist limit writer (not in the loadout applier; candidates: stores at
  `0x100275e1`, `0x10027a7a`, `0x1002f77e`, or set from the HUD/MGEO records); how c[10]
  moves the mech (physics `0x10019310`, 26 KB, or the animation duration at object +0x94);
  tick unit `DAT_1024ab4c`. (done: all three leads resolved in the rows below and §18)

## 2. Combat (src/combat.c, src/combat.h)

### Engine findings (3dfx MW2.DLL)
- Heat model `0x10015f50`, every frame per mech: heat c[0x26] += c[0x25] (heat added this
  frame) - c[0x27] (dissipation per tick, from `0x10041410`) x elapsed ticks, x2 while shut
  down (state 3); never below 0. Thresholds (float): > 65 "Heat level critical" (message 0,
  voice 75) while rising; > 80 automatic shutdown (message 1, voice 78) unless overridden;
  restart when < 65 and >= 724 ticks (~4 s) shut down; > 100 not overridden for 4525 ticks
  (~25 s) -> `0x10016280` = mech destroyed (kill table +1); > 100 overridden -> random ammo
  explosion ("BlowAmmo", `0x10015e10`).
- Message table `0x1024f144`: 12-byte entries {text, 0, voice sample}; shown by `0x10031870`
  (message number in ecx).
- The simulation has its own weapon table: `.data 0x1025a6d0`, 0x58-byte entries, extracted
  to src/wtable.h. Fire routine `0x100437a0`: ready when clock - last >= refire (+0x34);
  while firing, every `interval` (+0x38) ticks adds `heat` (+0x24) to c[0x25].
  Projectiles per volley at +0x0c. Damage is not in this table: next to find (projectile /
  hit code), along with the meaning of interval for beams vs. volleys, and ammo use.

| Item | Current value | Status | Lead |

Shell values are display figures and differ (e.g. ER LL 8 dmg / 6 heat, Gauss 18 dmg) |
| Refire / volleys | per-weapon state machine | **engine** (`0x100437a0`): recycle `refire` ticks after a volley, volley of `per_volley` projectiles one per `interval` ticks | weapon-fire routine (start from "Heat level critical" and weapon sound playback) |
| Heat dissipation | heat sinking x 0.000763 per tick (x2 below -30, 0.000687 above 50, from the planet), x2 shut down | **engine**; player difficulty factors known, normal difficulty used |
| Heat thresholds | warn 65; above 80 a shutdown sequence, shut down 1086 ticks later unless overridden (O); restart < 65 after 724 ticks; above 100: not overridden -> destroyed 4525 ticks after the sequence began, overridden -> 3-in-4000-per-tick ammo explosion (first bin with rounds; destroyed if none) | **engine** (`0x10015f50`, `0x100160c9`, `0x10015e10`) |
| To-hit / collision | projectiles fly at table speed for table life (with drop); each step is sampled at 4 points tested against posed part hulls (bounding sphere, then inside all face planes); location = the part's OBJL group | **engine** (`0x100103e0`, sphere `0x10029db0`, type-2 hull `0x1000d6b0` via `0x10010650`). Hull planes are rebuilt from the part polygons and oriented by the part centre (the engine stores its own planes). Collision types 0/5 (terrain), 1 (XZ box), 4 (none) apply to world objects; LOS and AI avoidance still use triangle tests |
| Missile hits | each missile is a projectile flown and collided like any other | engine method (see To-hit) |
| Hit location | part struck (OBJL group); CT hits redistributed to LT/CT/RT | **engine** (`0x100171d0`) |
| Damage / destruction | armour -> that location's structure, no transfer; rear armour (torsos) within +-90 deg; side torso takes arm; CT takes torsos, arms, head; head/CT = destroyed; 1st leg immobile, 2nd destroyed | **engine** (`0x100171d0`, `0x10016640`, `0x10044f60`); critical hits per engine: rand(5) rolls of rand(100) < 20, 12 = location lost, else a random slot: weapon destroyed; ammo bin explodes (rounds x per-round damage into the location's structure, rounds lost); leg actuators/hip/engine/gyro -10% speed (floor 40%), gyro also disables jumping; heat sink -1 sink; jump jet -1 jet; cockpit destroys the mech (`0x100167b0`). leg/hip/engine/gyro, heat sink and cockpit effects applied; arm actuators / sensors damage the player's cockpit displays (`0x1001e670`: each of 26 HUD components, rand(2 or 5) < ...) **engine** - no effect until the HUD exists; life support (hostile atmosphere) and jump jets applied |

## 3. AI and mission logic (src/msim.c, src/mtbl.c)

### Engine findings: the AI is a data-driven rule system
- Order dispatch `0x10014520` -> `0x10014700(order, behaviour, leader?)`: per object class 1-8,
  index tables (`0x1024baa8`, `0x1024bb68`, `0x1024bae8`, `0x1024bba8`, `0x1024bbe8`,
  `0x1024bb28`) pick an AI program for leader / follower; the program pointer is stored at
  object +0x11c (3 slots).
- Programs = the 9 AIT resources (DEFLT, F/L x DEFEND, DESTROY, LEAVE, RECON), loaded by
  `0x100141a0` into `0x101e8844[]`.
- Program layout: u16 count, count x {u16 situation code, u16 value} (searched by
  `0x10013d90`), then 12-byte rules starting with u16 action; recurring condition codes
  0x40, 0x42, 0x22, 0x25 with parameters such as range code -2 / -4 (the stored AI ranges,
  see GPS "250") or distances (250, 300, 350, 500, 1000).
  e.g. DESTROY / DEFEND programs: `07 00 01 42 fe ff 04 ...` (action 7, condition 0x42, range -2).
- AI states (debug table `0x1024b5d0`): 0 idle, 1 avoid, 2 target, 3 attack, 4 flee, 5 follow,
  6 recon, 7 patrol, 8 godirect, 10 rest, 11 shutdown, 12 dead. State set by `0x10013e00` ->
  `0x10013790` (object +0x14a), only if the program lists that state (`0x10013d90`).
- Program header = (state, rule count) pairs; rules follow, grouped by state in header order
  (DEFLT: 14 rules x 12 = 168 bytes, exact). Rule collector `0x10011e40` gathers the current
  state's rules from the 3 program slots (slot 2 first, duplicates dropped) into object +0x118.
- Rule (12 bytes): u16 condition (0-7, table `0x1024bc70`: 1 `0x10012b40`, 2 `0x10012bf0`
  target choice by range, 3 `0x10012a80`, 4 `0x10012a60`, 5 `0x10012a70`, 6 `0x10013090`,
  7 `0x10012fd0` range check with stored ranges -2/-1), then condition params (flag byte,
  operator byte, i16 range/distance); byte 6 = action (table `0x1024bc90`: 0 `0x100132a0`,
  1 `0x100132b0`, 2 `0x100132c0`, 3 `0x10013320`, 4 `0x100141e0`); byte 7 = new state;
  bytes 8-11 more params. Evaluator `0x10011d20`: first rule whose condition and action both
  succeed changes the state.
  e.g. DEFLT patrol: `07 00 01 42 fe ff 02 02 ..` = if condition 7 (range -2) then action 2,
  state -> target.
- Lancemate commands (`0x10014390`) use the same state numbers: 2 attack my target, 5 form on
  me, 7 (attack variant), 8 go to nav, 11 hold.
- Target references (16-bit): 0x01xx nav point, 0x02xx object, 0x04xx group; selectors
  0x2100 current nav, 0x2101 assigned nav (+0x16a), 0x2200 the player, 0x2201 my leader,
  0x2202 self, 0x4200 iterate enemies (`0x10013a50`). Range of a reference (`0x10013450`):
  nav point radius, objects/groups 100 m (10000 cm), minimum 1 m.
- Rule bytes 2-3 = condition target reference (e.g. 0x4201 enemies), 4-5 = range/distance
  (-2/-1 stored AI ranges, -3 unlimited, else metres), 6 = action, 7 = new state, 8-11 =
  further reference (e.g. bytes 9-10 0x2201 my leader in DEFLT follow).
- Conditions (return a target reference, 0 = false): 1 nearest enemy within range (`0x10012b40`);
  2 highest-scoring target (`0x10012bf0`, score = targeting code `0x1001d510` > 0);
  3 nearest enemy within the reference's range (`0x10012a80`, + bookkeeping); 4 passes the
  current target (always true); 5 always false - fires only on a posted event (object +0x144 =
  condition, +0x146 = target; how commands/orders cut in); 6 `0x10013090` state/visibility
  flag check on the target (`0x10013c40`, flag 2); 7 range check vs stored ranges
  (`0x10012fd0` -> `0x10012de0`).
- Actions (non-zero = commit the state change): 0 always; 1 clears counter +0x140; 2 saves
  (state, target) to +0x128 for return, counts in +0x140; 3 succeeds when +0x140 == 0;
  4 alerts the group leader (`0x10014270`) and returns 0 (never changes state itself).
- Per-state movement `0x100130f0` (implemented in src/ai.c): target -> steer to target, throttle
  by distance (`0x100134c0`); flee -> full throttle; follow/recon/patrol/godirect -> waypoint
  (`0x10014ff0` torso sweep), distance throttle, steering (`0x10013600`); else 80% throttle in mode 2.
- Firing (`0x100231a0`, implemented): fire only within +-10 deg of the aim line, at intervals of
  rand(GPS skill + 1) x 22 ticks (+0x158), extra rand(3) gate for non-player targets; torso
  twist input written directly each frame (instant within the MGEO limit).
- Events (`0x10014270`, implemented): action 4 posts (rule condition, current target) to the
  group leader if none pending; the evaluator then fires the matching rule without its condition.
- GPS per spawn (implemented): payload +8 skill, +10/+12/+14 ranges for codes -4/-1/-2 (0 -> 250 m);
  payload +16 pilot level 1-4 (+0x159) sets manoeuvre capability flags (+0x19e) in `0x1001ee30`:
  bit0/bit1 level<4, bit2 <3, bit3 mech and <6, bit4 <5, bit5 mech and <2, bit6 <6 (lower = better).
- Attack manoeuvres (`0x1001ea40`, +0x170): 13 manoeuvres, chosen by `0x1001f030` as a Markov
  chain over static tables (mechs `0x1024cdf0`, alternative `0x1024cf28`; other classes fixed
  rows `0x1024cef8` = 12, `0x1024cf10` = 1, `0x1024cee0` = 0): row = (manoeuvre, n, next options),
  next = options[rand(n)]; overrides: 4 needs conditions and twist, 3 -> 2 if twist limit < 10,
  5 skipped in one case. Manoeuvre functions: 0 `0x1001f950`, 1 `0x1001f9d0` (moves to points
  ~150 m, every 543 ticks), 2 `0x1001faf0`, 3 weave (+-450 turn, flip every 543 ticks;
  IMPLEMENTED as the attack movement), 4 `0x1001fdd0`, 5 `0x1001fe30`, 6 `0x1001fb90`,
  7 `0x1001fc40`, 8 `0x1001fc00`, 9 `0x1001fcc0`, 10 `0x1001ff70`, 11 `0x1001fe10`, 12 `0x1001ffd0`.
- Manoeuvres (implemented in src/ai.c): 0 close to 150 m, ends < 45 m (forces 10) or after
  rand*181+1448 ticks; 1 move near the target (40 m), 3620 ticks; 2 close to 55 m, ends at
  max(1, km/h x 0.12987) x 80 m; 3 = 2 + weave; 6 charge at full throttle, ends < 20 m;
  7 close to 30 m, ends < 30 m; 8 full speed (ends on contact); 10 full speed; 11 hold and aim;
  12 approach to 30 m (vehicles); 4/5/9 jump-jet manoeuvres. Every manoeuvre aims the torso and
  runs the fire decision; general limit 9050 ticks. Aim is at the target's current position
  (`0x10013be0`): the engine does not lead.
- (older note) the per-state behaviour
  (`0x10013680` state setup, `0x10012730` / `0x10012150` per-state movement), then implement
  the interpreter in src/ai.c and replace msim.c's steering.

| Sky/ground geometry, slots, tiling | as decoded | **engine** | - |
| Fog density unit (x0.1) | as decoded | **engine** (PowerVR x0.1 scale) | - |
| Fog curve | PowerVR PCX2 table fog, per pixel from the view depth z (game units): i = floor(K / z), K = 2560 / x, x = -log2(1 - d) with d the skygnd.par value MW2 passes to sgl_set_fog; fog f = PowerTable[i & 127] >> min(i >> 7, 9) (PowerTable[k] = 256 x 2^(-k/128), [0] = 255), i.e. about 2^(-i/128): none from i = 1152 (near), 50% at i = 128 (z = 20 / x: Golden Spade 57 m, Mirror Cage 239 m, Silent Thunder 936 m), never quite the fog colour; colour += (fog - colour) x f / 256 | **driver source** (Imagination's open-sourced Series 1 driver, MIT, github.com/powervr-graphics/PowerVR-Series1: dlcamera.c sgl_set_fog, rncamera.c fog shift, simulat3/hwsabren.c Fog, texas.c blend) + **engine** (MW2 PowerVR 0x1005ff50: sgl_set_camera foreground 50 x 0.1 = 5 -> Cd 0.5, vertices x 0.1 0x101a7580). The screen-rescale shifts cancel; the shift's 0..31 clamp and "negative = off" never bind at MW2's densities |
| Fog colour | mean(ground, sky, ambient term) (both enabled; mean of one colour + ambient otherwise), then x the scene light colour | **engine** (PowerVR `0x10060320`: (c_ground + c_sky + a) x 1/3, or x 1/2; `0x100608fc` multiplies by the light colour). The ambient term a = render-config +0x2a x 3/256 = the planet's LITE ambient level (PowerVR loader 0x10036d52 copies LITE +0x1c into config +0x2a; e.g. Jack 50 -> 0.59); the port now uses it for the fog colour and as the scene ambient (shade = base x min(1, ambient + N.L), SGL's ambient plus parallel light) |
| Sky drift rate (deliberate: 30 fps reference agreed with the user) | tile_sky x anim_rate added per frame (defaults 4 x 0.0001) | **engine** (`0x100277b0` reads skygnd.par; `0x10027af9` adds it once per sky draw, no time scaling). The original therefore drifted with the frame rate (cap = 181/N ticks, N from a config string, `0x10038982`); the port normalises to 30 fps - ASSUMED reference rate |
| Distance LODs (superseded: see §18 "Levels of detail") | always highest detail | simplification | switch distances are in object names (`_24000`) |

## 5. Data still unused

HUD sprites (`SHP`, 289), per-mech HUD records (`HUD`, 292 bytes: likely damage-diagram
rectangles), cockpit record (`CPIT`), overlays (`XYC`), MGEO (per-unit, likely hit boxes).

| Jets bar (open - see "Open items") | jump-capable mechs show a third bar "Jets" (fuel) at x 422-491; fill = fuel x (W + 1) / 0x712, the empty part RED bands (0x1001c750, session 11) | DONE; all three bars drawn as DOS: 15 px tall at 1024x768 in stripes (base 4 / light 4 / base 4 / dark 3), blue 0,0,199 / 0,48,215 / 0,0,125, green 0,211,0 / 0,255,85 / 0,125,0; the heat fill's yellow tones and the Jets bar's empty colour ASSUMED |

| Vision modes (superseded: see §18 "Image enhancement colours (complete)" and "Light amplification" (3D tint still open)) | l: "Light amplification engaged." - the scene becomes out = (16,32,8) + in x (0.5, 1.0, 0.6) (fitted to DOS colour pairs); w: "Image enhancement engaged." - black, polygons as lines, terrain red, the cockpit blue | DONE; line tones approximate; which actors are blue vs red (other mechs) ASSUMED blue |
| Pilot keys (superseded: see §15 "View controls in DOS", §18 "Autopilot", §20 "SHUTDOWN_MECH / STARTUP_MECH") | the original KEYBOARD.MAP: legs on Left / Right, torso tilt Up / Down, torso pan , . / End PgDn, throttle = - and keypad + -, presets 1-0; W / A / S freed for enhanced vision / autopilot / shutdown | DONE (held-key rates ASSUMED: throttle 0.6/s, tilt 30 deg/s, pan 45 deg/s); autopilot and shutdown keys NOT YET |
| Cockpit frames (superseded: see §18 "Cockpit frame (session 11f)" (full skeleton: IoU 0.96)) | DOS reference docs/reference/dos_kitfox_cockpit_1024.png (Instant Action, the Kit Fox selected; weapons LLASER / SPLAS / SSRM4 / xAC5): a window outline - a sill across the bottom centre joined by diagonals to the sides, and two diagonal beams across the top corners. Tested against it (DOS KITFOX geometry, 90 degree view, eye offsets +-2 m): the head piece with any culling (best IoU 0.19), the complement of the KF1WINSH panes (0.16) - neither. The mech records' cockpit chunks (DOS MW2.EXE 0x4efae..: CPTF, PITF, VPTF, HUDF, MGDF, EYEO) were traced: every mech names the same CPTF / VPTF "mw2mech" (the CPIT layout) and PITF "mw2pit" - a BWD record holding only an ANM2 chunk {0, 0x12, 1, 0xaf}, which the engine turns into a palette / texture cycle (0x10c30), not geometry; HUDF / MGDF name the mech's HUD and MGEO records; EYEO = the eye node (Kit Fox 16: inside the head shell, 65 cm behind its front). So the struts come from the mech's own model. The eye object is the piece's +0x44 (its root +0x40; pieces at 0xf8a40); the camera (FUN_000395d0, camera record DAT_00096efc) takes the attached object's world position (0x1df40) plus the record's +0x68 / +0x6c and, in one mode (DAT_0009620c == 0), halves the third coordinate - a lower eye tested (down to -5 m) scores worse (<= 0.08). Next: which of the player's polygons the renderer keeps in cockpit view (the object draw loop's test against the camera's attached piece). The view reversed along z scores worse (0.14). Earlier: DOS references captured for Timber Wolf, Marauder (hexagon + spokes), Summoner (sill + top edge), Mad Dog (diagonals), Elemental (open), Gargoyle (tall pillars), Hellbringer (side struts + sill), training mech (top beam, pillars, sill) | rule still OPEN: tested quantitatively against the DOS strut pixels (IoU) - whole eye piece, winding +/- , all parts, eye offsets +-2 m, the 0x4C / 0x61 colour sets, dropping near-plane polygons - none reproduces them (best IoU 0.35). The struts lie inside the head shell's coverage; the shell's winding is consistently outward (culling selects all or nothing); drawing its edges, or the DOS detail sets 1-3, also fails (best IoU 0.13 / 0.35). Two DOS captures of the same Timber Wolf start show DIFFERENT struts, so the eye moves relative to the cockpit with the mech's animation - the rest-pose comparison is the likely flaw. Next: capture struts frame by frame and compare against the animated pose |

Destroy nodes (kinds 1 / 2) thus take classes 1 / 2 (Pink: uplink, data-flow guns, comm arrays, mining buildings; not the walls, processors, dishes of class 4), protect (4) class 4, scan (8) class 8 (TNW5's Hip Actuators) | **engine** (0x1003ff60, 0x1003ddf0, 0x1000a1d0 / 0x10009a50 / 0x10009ab0) |

### Projectile objects and explosion light (tools/glview.c, src/glr.c)
| What | Port | Status |
|------|------|--------|
| Projectile model | chosen by the weapon table's +0x00 kind, as the engine's pool search (0x10043dc5 matches MW2_SHT1 BTHG kinds): 0-2 LASER1-3, 3 SRM / Narc MISSILE2, 4 LRM / Streak MISSILE1, 5 BULLET, 6 PPCSHOT1, 7 GAS0_0 (the gauss rifle); +0x14 = 0 (the flamer) launches no object | **engine** + game data (previously chosen by the visual field: SRMs showed LRM missiles, gauss a bullet) |
| PPC / gauss cards (superseded: see §18 "PPC / gauss") | camera-facing sprites (PPCSHOT1 scale 3 x 2, GAS0_0 scale 1) with the effect texture of their colour slot (0xb0 / 0xd0): bank 0 if present, else bank 1 (PPC: a9exp214, gauss: a9jump04) | slot from the models' colour; the bank rule and billboarding ASSUMED |
| Explosion light | the planet's LITE record (<4 letters>PLT1: {position x/y/z, ambient level (short), a camera-relative flag, a range}) is the scene light; a lighting effect type (0x1025b178 +0x10) moves it to the effect - the one nearest the camera - clears the flag and the range (a world-fixed light, no falloff), lowers the ambient level by 10 (outside 0..255 -> 64) and restores all at its end. Port: objects lit from the effect's position (the sky and ground layers keep the sun), ambient (level - 10) x 3/256 | **engine** (3Dfx 0x10045d00 / 0x10046490; PowerVR the same code at 0x1003e770 - which there only changes data the SGL parallel light was set from once, so the PowerVR edition showed no explosion light); field meanings from the LITE loaders (3Dfx 0x1003e0xx, PowerVR 0x10036d52) |

### Controls (tools/inputmap.h, tools/glview.c)
| What | Port | Status |
|------|------|--------|
| Bindings | the sim reads the game directory's GAMEKEY.MAP (commands; exact CTRL / ALT / SHIFT match) and INPUT.MAP (held controls: blocks of "+ / - device key" conditions, all must hold; several blocks of one control OR); without them the shipped files' bindings (tools/inputmap_defaults.h, checked against the install by test_inputmap) | **game data** |
| Key names (superseded: see "Cockpit Controls" row (INPUT.MAP rebuilt byte for byte with KEYBOARD.DLL names)) | plain cursor names are the keypad (LeftArrow = keypad 4, Home = 7, End = 1, Insert = 0, Delete = .), "Grey" names the cursor block and the keypad operators; NUMLock, KeypadEnter, Keypad5 | INFERRED from the files (e.g. legs_pan on LeftArrow and on GreyLeftArrow without Control, pilot_pan on GreyLeftArrow with Control) |
| Used (partly done: glances / rates §15 "View controls in DOS"; REVERSE_DIRECTION, dead zone, axis scales open - see "Open items") | every GAMEKEY command the sim implements; INPUT.MAP: throttle, legs, torso pan / tilt / resets, pilot pan / tilt / resets, glances, zoom, jump jets and the four directional jets, weapon fire / cycle / groups / group toggle / cycle group, the targeting set and next nav (once per press, any device); sticks (axes, buttons, hat) and the mouse (movement, buttons) through SDL | key handling **game data**; REVERSE_DIRECTION flips the throttle, glance angles (90 / 45 degrees), view pan / tilt / zoom rates, the stick dead zone (8%) and the axis scales ASSUMED |
| View controls in DOS (DOSBox, Instant Action, Nova) (open - see "Open items") | glances (keypad 7 / 9) turn only the eye - compass and radar stay; pilot pan (Ctrl + grey arrows) turns the view gradually while held and stays turned after release (0.15 s: a few degrees, 0.6 s: well round to the side); the legs turn about 61 degrees/s in place (one 0.35 s press of keypad 4 = 21 degrees, from a 24-step panorama). Measured with the targeted enemy's radar bearing against its bracket (radar ring fitted: centre 143,138, radius 122 px at 1024x768 = 1 km): glance right puts a target at bearing 52.6 deg 15 deg left of the view centre -> the glance is about 67 deg (port: 67; +-5 for the blip choice and the 90 deg view). Engine (0x1002e640): glances set pilot pan -70 / +70 (left / right) and pilot tilt 50 up / 40 down while held, and the first frame without one recentres - port now the same (the 67 measured agrees within its +-5). Key-driven controls (0x10039ee0): a held _plus / _minus key accelerates the control at 3 << shift per tick (velocity capped at half scale per tick, value -1..1), the velocity dropping to 0 on release; ranges from the control table 0x10256540 (pilot_pan -70..70, pilot_tilt -60..60, eyepoint_pan_delta -72..72, legs_pan_delta -1024..1024); pilot pan keeps its value after release (as captured). Port: legs and pilot pan / tilt ramp at 48.5 full-scale/s^2 (shift 5), which reproduces the DOS leg taps (0.2 s -> about 7.3 deg, 0.35 s -> 20.5; measured 7-9 / 21); legs stop on release (as captured). Zoom: control zoom_factor 1..4 (camera +0x18, the projection zoom: the view narrows as tan / zoom), measured in DOS from the radar view cone (a 0.12 s tap of Z: 1.00 -> 1.17, again -> 1.39; 1.5 s -> 4.0; Shift+Z 0.25 s: 3.97 -> 3.47; Ctrl+Z -> 1.0); port: the key ramp at 12.1 /s^2 (shift 3) on the -1..1 value mapped to 1..4, the radar cone narrowing with it | glances **engine**; ramp form **engine**, its rate fitted to **DOS captures** (each control's shift is set where the INPUT.MAP bindings are built: not traced) |
| Directional jets (superseded: see §18 "Directional jet drag") | while jetting and the legs not turning, the first held of left, right, forward, back pushes at the jets' thrust along it, no lift; on the ground the drift eases at 1/45 per tick, snapping to 0 under 0.0625 cm/tick | **engine** (0x10019310; session 11); airborne drag NOT decoded |
| Port extras kept | g: free camera; with no mouse in INPUT.MAP: the mouse read as MOUSE.MAP (see "Input: mouse") and the left button fires | port only (the old F = fire everything was removed: f is TARGET_FRIENDLY) |
| Cockpit Controls (open - see "Open items") | GIDDI CPC files (name, 16 devices, 4 levels x 37 slots x {type, device, flags Ctrl 1 / Alt 2 / Shift 4 / reverse 0x80000000, 0, a, b}); the INPUT.MAP writer (src/cockpitcfg.c) gives the installed INPUT.MAP byte for byte from KEYBOARD.CPC; inputs: keyboard = scan code - 1 with KEYBOARD.DLL's names, the other drivers' lists as their DLLs list them | **game data** + test_cockpitcfg; the merge of several devices' defaults (each fills the first free levels) and the jump-turn keys rule INFERRED |
| Shell dialog | FUN_000263d0: shell image 6 frame 2 at (172,165), lines from y 197 every 16 in font 0x20, buttons frames 0 / 1 centred on 320 - 30 (n - 1) + 61 k, y 276 | **engine**; DOS capture docs/reference/dos_flee_dialog.png: 0 pixels different |
| INSPECT_TARGET (open - see "Open items") | action 0x12 (the action table's entry; FEET_TO_TORSO is 0x11) sets input +0x3a; the target (a mech, or a structure targeted at the reticle) is marked for the player's side when within its radius + 200 m: "Inspection successful", else "Target is beyond inspection radius"; scan objectives (MTBL_K_SCAN) complete for a group's inspected mech or when a record's scan-class things (class bit 0x8, TNW5's "Hip Actuators" structure) are inspected; the target viewer then shows what the structure holds | **engine** (0x1001d5xx, 200 m = 0x102474d0, messages from the DLL) and **game data** (GT class 0x8); the radius (half a mech's height, half a structure's box diagonal) and the viewer's sub-name line ASSUMED; TARGET_AT_RETICLE falls back to the nearest structure within 5 degrees (ASSUMED) |
| Throttle presets | THROTTLE_STOP, _2 .. _9, _FULL = actions 0x1a .. 0x23 -> throttle (action - 0x1a) x 1/576 = 0, 1/9 .. 9/9 of full (full = 1/64) | **engine** (0x10038xxx; the port had (n-1)/8) and **DOS capture** (THROTTLE_6 = 5/9 on the speed bar) |
| Colour slot 0xf0 | never textured: a plain palette colour (colour >> 8). Used with every material except the textured hull one (0x98) - cockpit frames and many low-detail parts - while the mission maps put a clan crest in slot 0xf0 | **game data** (polygon statistics over the archive) |
| Cockpit field of view | 73.74 degrees vertical (90 horizontal at 4:3), narrowed by the zoom: 2 atan(tan(36.87) / zoom). (The controls round had a degrees conversion off by two - a 147-degree view - fixed with the frame work.) | **engine** (SGL camera zoom 1.333 x zoom_factor) |
| World block placement (BLKX) | {scale; pitch, yaw, roll in 16.16 degrees; x, y, z} places the next INCL'd record's BLK: turned about the centre of that BLK's box, then offset (p' = R(p - c) + t + c), composed with the enclosing blocks; a record with no BLK is not turned (ASSUMED, PINKSCN1's second pinkscri) | **engine** 0x10024e90 (x 1/65536), 0x10024ba0, 0x1002f070 / 0x1002eb50; **DOSBox** ORANSCN1 pump station size and bearing, BROWSCN1 Comm. Unit long side on (docs/reference/blkx_rotation_brow_dos_vs_port.png); test blkx_block_yaw |
| Missile lock and homing (superseded: see §20 "AI missile lock") | engine 0x10044560 per tick for the selected weapon: guided (table +0x18: LRMs, Streaks, Narc), the selected target within the weapon's +0x3c..+0x40 range window and 16 degrees of the aim both ways -> on target (0x8000); held 0x16a ticks (2 s) -> locked (0x80). Guided shots fired while locked take the target (0x100437a0) and steer each tick with acceleration unit(target - p) - unit(v) (0x10045910) until it dies. AI mechs: locked after 2 s with their target in range (ASSUMED) | **engine** |
| Reticle states | 0x10022710: weapon not ready RCLINOP; guided: locked RCLGLOC, on target RCLPLOC, else RCLNOLK; SRMs: RCLLOCK on target, else RCLNOLK; others: RCLTGT on target (target in range and within 3 degrees), else RETICLE | **engine** |
| Movie sound | Smacker audio track 0 (Huffman-coded DPCM, 8/16-bit, mono/stereo, as the multimedia wiki / ffmpeg describe it), decoded whole and mixed as one voice; frames paced to the clock | format **public**; checked: lengths equal the video (MWOLAND 72.8 s), lag-1 correlation 0.99 |
| Start view | missions start in the cockpit (F1 / COCKPIT_VIEW toggles the chase view) | original behaviour |
| Sound mixing | voices interpolated linearly from 11025 Hz, 2.5 ms ramps at both ends of one-shots, a busy mixer drops new sounds instead of cutting playing ones, same-sound bursts capped (2 per 0.1 s, 4 playing) | port (the clicks and buzz reported on macOS) |
| Explosion light | off by default (as the PowerVR edition, which never showed it); MW2_FXLIGHT=1 for the 3Dfx behaviour | user report: terrain flickering light / dark |
| Target bracket (superseded: see the later "Target bracket" row and §18 "Target bracket") | far / small targets: TGTOFFE (the DOS captures' rounded square), near: TGTGTE - engine 0x10022ea0 picks TGTOFF* when the projected target fails its size test; the switch at the bracket's own height ASSUMED | **engine** + DOS capture |
| Game speed | mw2port.cfg speed= (0.5-1.5, default 0.85) scales the simulation clock | user request (the original ran slower than real time on period PCs) |

DOSBox sustained-fire captures match; the automatic shutdown says "Shutdown sequence initiated" (message 3, voice 15) | user report (DOS); the 70 % ASSUMED |

| AI keeps clear of the player | no special rule: the avoidance probes (0x100201c0 -> 0x100103e0) test the whole collision list, so the player and the other mechs (4.5 m circles in the port) are obstacles like buildings; avoidance runs in states 2, 4-8, not 3 | **engine** (session 11; the 15 m rule removed) |
| Ground-plane polygons | any polygon lying in the ground plane (world y within 2 cm of 0, e.g. a building's footing / shadow) is raised 3 cm in the renderer, so it no longer flickers against the ground | user report |
| Autopilot target | the current nav point: the first open, shown REACH objective whose target is a nav point (objectives aimed at areas are skipped - the first such had made it -1 and the autopilot switched itself off), else the mission's first NAV point | user report |

## 17. Editions (Combat Variables RENDERER, mw2port.cfg edition=; session 11)

The shell passes the chosen edition's archive, texture kind, sky file and fog file to the simulation (MW2_EDITION). Gameplay
is the same code for all. Files: 3d/models.prj (3Dfx), 3d/textures.prj (ATi), 3d/skygnd.par (3Dfx), 3d/ati/skygnd.par,
3d/s3/MW2.PRJ + skygnd.par, 3d/pvr/MW2.PRJ + skygnd.par; DOS: the disc's MECH2/MW2.PRJ.

DOS shading (glr u_dos): colour word bits 12-14 type, 8-11 ramp, 0-7 intensity C or bitmap; type 0 / 2 unlit index
(w >> 4) & 0xff; 1 / 4 lit: ramp x 16 + level; 5-7 bitmap (low byte) and 3 bitmap ((w >> 4) & 0xff, the scrub / effect
cards) through LUMA[level][texel]; level = ((C >> 1) x ((f x (128 - amb) >> 7) + amb)) / 0x440, f = 127 cos(light,
normal) (two-sided: abs - the winding not traced), minus floor(depth / LITE +24), clamped 0-15; LITE {+8 x, +12 y, +16 z, (open - see "Open items")
+20 ambient, +22 directional, +24 fog distance}; palette = PAL id PALG[0] (JACK: 54 JACK_NI - JACK is a night mission in
DOS); LUMA id from the planet's LTBL (2 STANDARD); sky palette 0xE0, ground 0xEF, flat. ASSUMED / NOT YET: Gouraud type 4
(session 11) DONE since: Gouraud type 4 - each vertex's DOS field a is a baked index (ramp | intensity), drawn ramp + i x
(L + 1) / 16 with L the polygon's level, interpolated (0x3bb80); the horizon - the window filled sky 0xE0 where the view
ray's world y >= 0, else ground 0xEF, with a haze band above the line of HRZM +4 x width / 320 pixels fading 0xEE -> 0xE0
(0x3c000, 0x3a050; PLNT +68 switches it off); the time-of-day palette - t = (INIT +8 + clock s) % PLNT +12, groups dawn /
day / dusk / night at 5 / 7 / 17 / 19 hours -> PALG slots 4 / 0 / 4 / 8, linear 6-bit fades of 2730 / 2730 / 3640 / 2730
ticks, checked every 0x71c ticks, the first over 0x16c ticks from slot 0 (0x14ee0, 0x14f80, 0x3eae0). Gouraud vertices with
a < 48 (unlit, 2 %) are drawn rescaled like the rest - ASSUMED. (open - see "Open items")

## 21. Session 11l - automated sweeps (user request: find the bugs before the player does)

Three harnesses now exercise the game end to end: `make sweep MW2=<install copy>` (tools/sweep_objectives.c: every mission played by an invulnerable scripted pilot through its objectives), `make sweep_ai` / `make sweep-walk` (tools/sweep_ai.c, tools/sweep_walk.py: AI and player movement, stalls named by their blocker), `make test_sequences` (tests/sequences/run_sequences.py: 46 scripted action sequences through the real in-mission program, PASS / FAIL). missionsim writes MW2MSN.CFG / MW2CAR.CFG into MW2_INSTALL_DIR: point it at a copy.

| Mech-mech contact in 3D (supersedes "Player collision" / "AI collisions" contact rules; its damage rule superseded below: "Mech-mech contact damage: the mover only") (open - see "Open items") | every unit is a sphere (MGEO radius, centre_h above the feet); a move touches another when the centres come closer than r1 + r2 and closer than before (vertical motion included - a jumping / falling mech can land on another). On top (normal y > 0.7071) with the feet within 10 m of the terrain: landed, no damage to either, set down beside it. Otherwise the mover takes collision damage by the contact normal (y < -0.866: head x max(1, 2 x other tons / own tons); > 0.7071: half to each leg; else arm 45-135 degrees to a side, else that side's torso) at the engine's speed \|(dvx, dvy)\| (relative velocity; the engine copies the other's vz over its own, so the z difference never counts); the other takes the mirrored hit (port rule, once per contact) except when landed on. Airborne the mover bounces along the normal at max(1, \|(dvx, dvy, other vz)\| / 2) cm/tick (a mech dropped dead centre bounces down to rest on the other's sphere); walking: pushed back 60 cm at -30 % speed. Collision Damage (MW2DIF +3, shell default 1 = on) off: no contact damage. Port simplifications: the set-down beside (engine: stood on the terrain at the projected point, pushed out the next tick, a small wall hit possible), the other's mirrored hit, and once per contact (engine: every contact tick, separation by the bounce) | **engine** 0x1000b5e0 / 0x1000ba20 (sphere test, projection, bounce, the +0x108 copy), 0x10019c2c..0x10019d84 (landed test, 0x1000c160 vs 0x1000c3c0), 0x1000c1f0 (locations, head ratio from controller +0xe4 = MEK tonnage); shell default MW2DIF {0,0,1,1,1,1,0,0} (mw2shell 0x7d40c); user report (Jenner landing on the player) |

Criticals 5300 / 5350 / 5400 / 5450 roll every instrument at rand(10) < 2, critical 5700 at rand(10) < 5 (0x1001e670 flag, call 0x10016e33); a picked instrument's level rises by one (no cap; reset at mission start). Only three instruments read the level: 0 the radar - level > 0 turns light amplification off and refuses it (0x10007900 / 0x10007a50); its window static (0x10004710) needs the window descriptor's animation slot (+0x78 -> +8), which is -1 and never written, so the radar never fuzzes; 2 the bottom-right window, only in its viewport modes (rear / down / weapon, DAT_10250520 3-5 - not the damage outline or H T A L); 13 the target viewer (only while it has something to show). For 2 and 13 per drawn frame (30 fps ASSUMED): level 1 - static showing: rand(10) < 7 clears it, this frame still static; clear: rand(10) < 3 sets it, this frame normal (static on ~30 % of frames, mean run 1.4 frames); level 2 normal; 3+ static for good. The static replaces the window's contents (viewport label included; the target's name / range below the box are other instruments and stay): SHP SNOWCLR / SNOWCLR6 / SNOWCLRK (id 0xaf + the resolution set, 10 frames, 52x34 / 101x78 / 163x126, opaque), ANM2 slot 0 from the cockpit's MW2PIT record {flags 0, 18 ticks per frame, type 1, 0xaf}, looping from its first draw at the window's top-left. | Mech-mech contact damage: the mover only (supersedes the mirrored hit and "once per contact" in "Mech-mech contact in 3D") | a contact damages only the unit whose movement made it (0x10019d6a -> 0x1000c160(self, other): ECX = the mover, the other only supplies its velocity), on every contact tick (no latch: the 0x1007c338 latch only gates the player's clang, cleared by a tick without contact); the damage needs \|(dvx, dvy)\| above 3.069 cm/tick, so a unit resting / landed on another (landed branch 0x10019c4e: no damage; a resting bounce under the threshold) takes nothing. A standing unit is hit only by its own contacts (the port's sphere test needs closing motion, so a mover walking into a standing unit damages only the mover). | Frame rate | the simulation runs on elapsed time (182 ticks a second); the engine's per-frame effects (walking heat, cook-off roll, wreck explosions, display static, sky drift, eject spin, fade) are normalised to the 30 fps reference, so the frame rate (vsync, 60 / 120 / 144 Hz) changes nothing in play; each frame's time is passed to the simulation with its millisecond fractions carried over (truncating them had run the game up to 13 % slow at 144 fps) | port |

Gait (walk / run) and walking heat by c[0x13]; leg turn rate by c[0xb]. The AI's reverse flips the drive at once (its throttle stays positive); the player's throttle is signed (reverse at half). Port simplifications: feet kept on the ground (the engine's lift and hop not modelled); positions kept to the sub-cm; AI units keep no across-heading velocity; the 3Dfx float math (DOS's approximate distance max + (mid + min) / 4 gives a faceted contact shape: DOSBox TNJ6 ~5 s at ~40 fps vs the port's 10.5 s) | **engine** 0x1000b5e0, 0x1000ba20 (0x1000bc93 vz copy, 0x10247150 0.5, 0x1024713c 1.0), 0x10019310 (0x102473b4 1/45, 0x102473d0 0.0625); DOS 0x1fca0 / 0x200d0; **DOSBox** TNJ1 MCGA cycles 40000 ~32 fps: 8.0 s; ~60 fps 4.6 s; SVGA ~5 fps ~1.5 s; test tnj1_slide_round_instructor |
| AI torso sway / aim (refines "AI aim / aim range": the eye's world yaw) | the controller sets the torso node's (first THNG node = the twist node, every mech) whole local rotation every frame - pitch, twist, roll 0 - so the walk's relative rotation keys on it never add up: no torso sway; the AI's eye ray points along legs + lagged twist (the port had added the keys: the Gargoyle's ray swung +-4 deg yaw, Battlemaster / Hellbringer / Summoner +-2.6, Firemoth pitch). AQUASCN1 standing, DOS edition: time-to-death 29-60 s, mean 44.2 (9 AI seeds; was 32 s to > 100 s); first hit ~13 s from ~650 m. DOSBox: 33-52.5 s, mean 43.1 (7 runs), first damage ~13.5 s, AI approach 19.7 / 24.8 m/s (port 21 / 25). GOLDSCN1 first damage -> death: DOSBox 14 / 25 / 34 s, port 8.5-38 s (mean 28). Fire gate (0x100231a0: every rand(skill + 1) x 22 ticks, bearing within 10 deg of the lagged twist, weapon check, rand(3) for non-player targets), twist servo (0.6 s AI / 0.2 s player), manoeuvres traced and unchanged | **engine** 0x1001a180 -> 0x1000f990 / 0x1002f070 / 0x1000f8f0, 0x1000dec0 -> 0x1000f9d0, 0x100190d0, 0x10044950, 0x100231a0, 0x1001f030; DOS 0x2e860 / 0x266e0 / 0x2c880; **DOSBox** radar + H T A L; tests aqua_time_to_death, ai_aim_eye_ray |
| Area damage sources (refines "area damage 0x10046700") | damaging effect types (0x1025b168 +0x18): 3, 4, 6, 7, 12, 13, 17, 19-22; radius = the effect's model radius + the unit's; ticks / 256 per tick on locations 2-8, half on the head; option MW2DIF byte 2 (Splash Damage, default on). Besides shot impacts: the death explosion (0x1001be40, the first 0x0d at the unit), class 0xb0 things exploding (0x0d), an exploding ammunition bin (0x100167b0: effect 7 at the location's node, 0x1000fec0). Not ported: the nuke blast (0x10047020 / 0x10047130, order flag 0x1000); destroyed units taking area damage x 2 (no visible effect); 0x10029890's adjustment of the bin's node point | **engine** 0x10046490, 0x10046700, 0x10045c30 / 0x10045d00, 0x1001be40, 0x100167b0, 0x1000fec0; tests death_explosion_area_damage, ammo_critical_explosion, splash_area_damage |
| Contact moves at the 30 fps reference (refines "Mech-mech contact while walking" and "Terrain / object impact") | after a move touches a mech or a wall, the player's and AI's moves (drive, move, contact, impact) run in 1/30 s steps with the remainder carried, until 10 steps in a row touch nothing; free walking is one move per frame. TNJ1 slide 7.5 s at 30 fps, 7.8 at 60 / 144; TNJ4 wall slide 55 cm/s at 30 / 60 / 144. Positions keep their sub-cm part | **engine** per-frame rules 0x1000ba20 / 0x1000b5e0 / 0x10019310 (frame-rate dependent: DOSBox TNJ1 8 s ~32 fps, 4.6 s ~60); port: the 30 fps reference rule; tests tnj1_slide_frame_rates, tnj4_wall_slide |
| Wall slide (refines "Terrain / object impact") | velocity after a wall hit kept along the heading (r x \|v\| x -0.25 projected; across part dropped); a refused second try keeps the bounced position when the same object refused it (terrain counts as one), else the unit stays (type 1 walls: stays); x -0.5 either way. TNJ4 S_1BQDAS heading -24.5: 55 cm/s | **engine** 0x1000b5e0 (0x1000b884..0x1000b9e9), 0x10019a6a, DOS 0x1fca0; **DOSBox** MCGA TNJ4 heading -18.5 ~20 fps: ~60 cm/s (the traced full-vector rule gives ~28, this rule ~55) - something untraced keeps the original faster |

## 23. The Titanium look (2026-10-08; the user's Titanium disc, 16-bit build)

| Behaviour | Now | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Titanium engine | the 16-bit build renders with mercsw.dll (renderer plugins; the BATTLE.DLL beside it is a 1997 8-bit DirectDraw engine and the shell only checks the CD for `:MW2\BATTLE.DLL`) | INFERRED: mercsw 0x1001cd93 loads the plugins, reads particle.PID, the full SKYGND.PAR (0x1009e9e0) and the "1.2" textures; MW2SHELL 0x100223b0 is a CD check; Mw2win.exe (compressed) not unpacked |
| Titanium texture format | "1.2x", u16 type, w, h, n, n x BGRA, w x h indices; type 0 -> 16-bit RGB with alpha ignored (RGB565 here; the screen format decides, INFERRED); other types -> ARGB4444 with alpha 15 - (a >> 4), inverted | **engine**: mercsw 0x10002867-0x100029cc (load), 0x1000131e (type > 0 -> Render type 8); dx5draw 0x10006200, 0x1000634f, 0x10006713 |
| Titanium plain CEL records | never textures in the 16-bit build (the loader keeps only "1.2"); treated as missing | **engine** mercsw 0x10002867 |
| Titanium one-colour records | 45 type-0 records with a one-entry palette (tails of A9EXP2xx/6xx, A9EXBRxx, A9EXPFxx) count as missing | ASSUMED (placeholders; the 3D editions' effect tables play to those frames) |
| Titanium filtering | bilinear min and mag, no mips, sky and ground too (superseded: see §24 - TEXTURES TITANIUM takes the renderer's filtering) | **engine**: dx5draw 0x100034a0 (D3DFILTER_LINEAR), no mip caps 0x100056ae; Glide grTexFilterMode(1, 1) zglide 0x10001ad0 |
| Titanium effects blending | textures of type > 0 alpha-blended SRCALPHA / INVSRCALPHA, not additive; additive (type 7) only for particle.PID particles, not ported | **engine**: dx5draw 0x100044ff, 0x100035ab; mercsw 0x1004d7ba |
| Titanium SKYGND.PAR | one sscanf per line; '#' and after dropped; tile_ground / 128, ground_max x 2, density Z x 10, skyAnim / 181; unread fields 0 (the engine keeps the previous mission's) | **engine** mercsw 0x10053b00-0x10054097 |
| Titanium fog | per vertex a = (Z ? 1 - min(1, z / Z) : 1) x f; f = 1, or with density Y != 0 and eye height h > 0: t = Y / h (Y > 0) or -Y x h (Y < 0), f = t < M ? M : min(t, 1); h <= 0 and Y < 0: f = M. Port: per pixel; h = eye y in cm | **engine** mercsw 0x10053474, 0x10001b53, 0x1006a851; units cm INFERRED |
| Titanium clear colour | sky x g + fog x (1 - g), g = f (x Y / 50000 when Y > 0), clamped M..1; Y = 0: sky= as is | **engine** mercsw 0x1006a989-0x1006aadf |
| Titanium ground | Titanium map slot 0x4a; quad half size ground_max x 4 (raw), UV = world / (half / (tile_ground / 128)); none when tile_ground <= 0 | **engine** mercsw 0x100559eb, 0x1005545b, 0x1005406f |
| Titanium sky dome | the SKYDOME object (TL1SKYDM: clouds, mountain ring, moon, sun) with the Titanium map's slots; UV = texel / (size - 1); clouds drift skyAnim / 181 per tick, wrapped at 1. Drawn centred on the eye's x, z, no depth, unfogged, alpha-blended, polygons in slot order | **engine**: UV 0x10001477, drift 0x1005580d / 0x1000127c; placement, order and no-fog ASSUMED; the heading rotation of the cloud UVs (0x100013b0) not reproduced |
| Titanium textures by name | Titanium first; misses from the mission's texture archive (ATi). Titanium has 51 of the 418 names the 3D editions' maps use (it renamed most mech / building textures), 597 of the ATi archive's 1404 names (580 effect frames) - so mechs and buildings keep the ATi textures | port choice (measured) |

## 24. Look settings: RENDERER and TEXTURES (2026-10-11; user design change)

| Behaviour | Now | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Look settings | two settings replace the one edition list: RENDERER (enhanced = default 3Dfx look + PowerVR fog, dos, 3dfx, ati, s3, pvr, mga; no Rendition - user) and TEXTURES (original / titanium). mw2port.cfg renderer= / textures=; the old edition= is read (edition=titanium -> renderer=enhanced textures=titanium) and no longer written; textures= with a value other than original / titanium stays the texture-archive path override. TEXTURES is locked to ORIGINAL with the DOS renderer (palette-indexed archive) and without the titanium/ folder. Both switch live in a mission (DOS stays a shell choice) and are saved for the next mission | port choice (user request) |
| TITANIUM with a renderer | the Titanium archive's textures by name, the renderer's own texture archive for the names it lacks (mechs, buildings); the Titanium SKYGND.PAR, sky dome and ground; the renderer's filtering, lasers and light handling (the Titanium plugin's own bilinear / no-mip filtering is not taken) | port choice |
| TITANIUM fog per renderer | renderers that fog (ENHANCED, POWERVR) draw the Titanium fog (mercsw vertex fog 0x10053474 / 0x1006a851 with its SKYGND.PAR fog=, Z, Y, M) in place of the PowerVR densities; 3DFX / ATI RAGE / S3 VIRGE / MATROX MYSTIQUE no fog, the clear colour then sky= as it is (the engine's Y = 0 case, 0x1006a989); never the PowerVR veil over the dome | port choice |
| TITANIUM effects blending | alpha-blended (SRCALPHA / INVSRCALPHA) with every renderer: the engine picks Render type 8 by the texture's type, not the plugin | **engine** mercsw 0x1000131e; dx5draw 0x100044ff |
| Combat Variables layout | RENDERER (y 239) and TEXTURES (y 258) head the DETAIL group: the DIFFICULTY group's artwork drawn 20 px up (rows 199 / 219) into the gap under the VOICE slider, the DETAIL heading moved to the RENDERER row; rows stay 19-20 px apart; a locked TEXTURES drawn grey (110) | port layout (the original's widget table 0x7d09c has neither row) |
| In-mission GRAPHICS menu | Textured Sky, Textured Ground, Renderer, Textures, Accept on the sixth row; a locked Textures drawn dim (85 grey), passed over by the arrows and its digit | port addition in place of "Resolution 800x600" (0x10265570; commit 0x10066520 empty) |

| Titanium type-1 shade | L = LITE ambient + max(0, I×gi/128): the LITE vector (directional or point), or a point light at the eye for 888888×3 (0x100ad320 → 0x1009e954); 999999×3 means no term. Then L −= (depth/range)>>4 when range > 1e-7 (depth = the deepest corner; the object flag 0x10106c50 avg/min/+2^28 rules not ported), clamped ≥ 0. With Multiple Lights and L < 0x80, each dynamic light whose box holds a corner adds its unscaled I, until 0x80. level = L×((w&0xff)>>1)/0x440, 0..15; recomputed per frame. Both LITE flags are sticky across missions in the engine; the port resets them per mission. No Titanium mission uses 888888 or 999999, and 16×range exceeds every VIEW far distance. | **engine** 0x1002f5e3..0x1002f820, 0x10056f13, 0x10071631..0x100716f6, 0x100575d3..0x1005761f, 0x1002eb36 |
| Titanium billboards | 0x1004ef4c mode 0: corners 0 (T.x−dx, T.y−dy), 1 (F.x−dx, F.y+dy), 2 (T.x+dx, F.y−dy), 3 (F.x+dx, T.y+dy), with dx = (F.y−T.y)/2 and dy = (F.x−T.x)/2 in screen pixels (square only for an upright edge); UVs (0,0)(0,1)(1,1)(1,0); the u/v < 0 flip swaps equal u's (no-op); bitmap from bank 0; colour 0xc8. The diffuse alpha 0x80 is unused: dx5draw never sets TEXTUREMAPBLEND, so D3D's default MODULATE takes a type > 0 texture's alpha, and type 2 does not blend. | **engine** 0x1004ef4c..0x1004f3ba, 0x1009dfe0, 0x10001375; dx5draw 0x10002b60, 0x100034xx..0x1000373b |
| Titanium dynamic lights / granularity | Static lights per vertex (0x1002ecaa), interpolated (Gouraud, dx5draw SHADEMODE). Dynamic lights per polygon: a polygon is lit when one of its corners is in the light's ±radius box (edges included). Then, corner by corner, a running sum S starting at the last corner's static colour, + colour × N·L/\|L\| (when > 0), is added to the corner, capped at 255. Port: CPU per-vertex colours each frame (glr `ti_colours`). Projectile light: the volley's first projectile, radius 10000, 0.2 grey or palette[v0.u]/128 for a kind-4 model. | **engine** 0x1002ecaa..0x1002f5d5, 0x1002eb36, 0x1006c470, 0x1006cd6c..0x1006ced3; tests titanium_lights, titanium_smoke_and_lights |

Cards (PPC, gauss) keep the 3D editions' pool slots. | **engine** 0x10072a7d, 0x1006ca99, 0x10072b06; test titanium_kind2_pool |

| Thing targeting rules (supersedes the open item) | for the player, a thing is refused when destroyed (flag 4), when it has neither 0x400 nor 0x1000 while DAT_1024c8b0 is 0, when it has 0x800, or when it is beyond 1750 m without 0x1000. DAT_1024c8b0 is 0 at load; t/r/e/f/n clear it, q sets it after its own check, Ctrl+t leaves it. So a plain structure needs a second q (or one q after q / Ctrl+t). t/r cycle units, then flagged things, then wrap; with no target they start at the first unit going forward. The upkeep drops a thing target that no longer passes | **engine** 0x1001d2d0, 0x1001d870, 0x1001cca0, 0x1001db60, 0x1001d510 (DOS 0x96290 / 0x2ab98); **DOSBox** SILVSCN1 hangar q/q/t/q/q/Ctrl+t/q, AMY_SCN1 t ×10; tests target_q_twice, target_things_cycle |

| DOS probe broad phase (0x3a745) | an entry is a candidate if the probe start is inside its sphere, else the 64-bit dot product dx*sx + dy*sy (+ dz*sz with add / adc) must not carry (jae 0x3a7de): with signed products the carry fires when the partial sums differ in sign and the total is >= 0, or both are negative - a thing ahead is seen only if its centre is ahead in x AND z of the probe direction. The probe end point is right (matrix = the unit's pose) | **DOSBox** patched-EXE readouts through the kph display: matrix translation - unit position = 0, z axis = heading; 100 / 120 m step-0 probe 0 until 232 m from Beta, 1 after; probe raised 5 m: same; hit node sphere (-140550, 169800) r 7650 = port S_1BQDAS; jae -> jmp: hits from range, autopilot wanders off course (docs/reference/autopilot_probe_broadphase_dos.jpg). The weapon step runs before the AI in the frame (0x1001a180) and acts on edges: pressed with the latch (+0x10c bit 1) clear -> latch, the selected weapon (+0xac) starts its volley if ready (state 1); released with the latch set -> unlatch and the selection moves to the next weapon still there (0x10043f80 with 0). So an AI fires one weapon per press and goes through its weapons in turn; a repeating weapon whose volley ends with the trigger down fires again after its refire while still selected. The port had kept the weapon that fired selected: the Stormcrow's UAC/20 (always ready, rand(1)) fired at every decision and emptied 10 rounds within ~10 s of 400 m. | DOS Gouraud dither | Type 4 polygons (cockpit, mechs, buildings) are filled as MW2.EXE 0x59e77 does. Vertices are projected to whole pixels with `((p<<s)/z+2)>>2` (0x3dfb7) and shaded `c = ramp·16 + i(L+1)/16` (16.16). Rows run from the top vertex (the last with the smallest y) down a backward and a forward chain; each edge starts at `x<<16+0x8000`, `c+0x8000` and steps by dx/dy, dc/dy (truncated). The span is stepped per pixel pair, `s = (cR−cL)/max(w>>1,1)`. The even pixel (from the span's unclipped left end) gets +0x7fff and the odd +0; they swap every row from the polygon's top. So `idx = (cL + ⌊k/2⌋s + (k+r even ? 0x7fff : 0)) >> 16`: a 1-px checkerboard of n/n+1 where the fraction is under 0.5, flat n+1 above, with the phase per polygon. Flat (0x59180) and textured (0x5b549, 0x3fb20) polygons don't dither. The port replays it in integers per output pixel at any size (glr `dos_gouraud`; GL's near plane, not DOS's 0x3de4e). Port option `dos_shading=smooth` (not the original; formerly `dos_sky`): colours blended at c + 0.25. Not in the port: Gouraud off (DAT_00097024 = 0 → flat 0x59180) and the mode-4 drawer 0x12690. | **engine** MW2.EXE 0x3bd08–0x3bd75, 0x12723, 0x3dfb7, 0x59e77 / 0x5a01f–0x5a320 / 0x5a5c4 / 0x5a5eb–0x5a71a. **DOSBox** RUSTSCN1 / YELLSCN1 1024×768: top bar 98% of pixels identical (before 22%), mean equal (was a step darker); the rest are cockpit edges 1 px off. Test `dos_shading_dither`. |

  in DOSBox); TNJ4 / TNJ1 start: the trainer stands dead ahead in the port while in DOSBox it has moved off. The nav reach
